みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

TSMC will host its 3Q26 analyst meeting on 15 October. We expect a 
strong finish to 2026 with a sustained growth outlook into 2027 for 
TSMC as demand broadens across AI GPUs/AI ASICs, networking 
and server CPUs. In particular, the stronger CPU demand driven by 
agentic AI represents a key change over the past year. We forecast 
2026E/2027E revenue growth of 42.0%/36.9% YoY (USD terms), 
with GM at 67.2%/67.5%. We also raise our 2027E/2028E CapEx 
forecasts to US$85bn/US$98bn (vs. US$78bn/US$82bn 
previously) as we expect TSMC to further ramp up its capacity 
investment to support longer-term customer demand. 
Strong sequential growth into 4Q26 with modest N2 ramp dilution 
We expect TSMC’s 3Q26E revenue to grow 15.3% QoQ, followed 
by a further 11.0% QoQ increase in 4Q26E (both in USD terms), 
supported by continued strength in AI/HPC demand. On 
profitability, we model GM edging down to 67.5%/67.3% in 
3Q26E/4Q26E, vs. 67.7% in 2Q26, reflecting dilution from the 
ongoing N2 ramp.  
Momentum sustaining into 2027, supported by broader AI demand 
We expect TSMC’s revenue to grow 36.9% YoY (vs. 32.0% previously) 
in USD terms in 2027E, supported by sustained demand for AI 
accelerators and networking, alongside an increasingly strong 
contribution from server CPUs. At our Communacopia + Technology 
conference, management highlighted that lower token costs should 
stimulate greater AI consumption, while discussions with customers 
and US CSPs has reinforced confidence in demand and 
infrastructure readiness. We believe these trends support continued 
strength in leading-edge demand, particularly for both N2 and N3, 
where management expects supply to remain insufficient despite 
further expansion.

### Page 3

On the supply side, we expect capacity additions, N5-to-N3 conversion, and 
productivity gains to support higher wafer output, while continued CoWoS expansion 
helps enable associated wafer shipments. We model GM improving modestly to 67.5% in 
2027E (vs. 67.2% in 2026E), supported by higher pricing, favorable product mix, 
sustained high utilization and continued productivity gains. 
Higher CapEx to support longer-term expansion 
We maintain our 2026E CapEx forecast at US$64bn, while raising our 2027E/2028E 
forecasts to US$85bn/US$98bn (vs. US$78bn/US$82bn previously), to incorporate 1) 
cost inflation from equipment vendors, and 2) initial spending associated with the 
potential new Texas expansion; however, we expect the new site to only enter mass 
production beyond 2032. For capacity, we continue to model our end-2027E/2028E 
N3/N2 capacity at 200kwpm/140kwpm and 220kwpm/200kwpm, respectively. 
Other key areas in focus for the upcoming analyst meeting 
Long-term AI growth outlook: We believe the broadening of AI demand across 
accelerators, CPUs and networking further supports TSMC’s long-term growth 
opportunity. We would look for updated comments from management on the key 
assumptions underpinning its AI outlook through 2030, where demand drivers have 
strengthened most over the past year, and the factors that could materially change this 
outlook. 
Longer-term CapEx outlook and US expansion: With customer demand continuing to 
drive investment in leading-edge capacity and advanced packaging, we would look for 
management to provide further direction on its longer-term CapEx trajectory and the 
demand visibility supporting these investment decisions. We would also welcome 
updated comments on whether customer demand could lead to further expansion in the 
US beyond current Arizona site, and the potential timing and scale of such investments. 
Foundry competition and technology leadership: We would look for management’s 
perspective on the evolving competitive landscape, including Intel, Samsung and 
Terafab, and the implications for TSMC’s longer-term positioning. In particular, we would 
welcome further discussion of its technology leadership, and whether management 
expects this gap to widen or narrow across upcoming process generations.

Forecast changes 
We have revised up 2026/27/28E earnings by 1%/7%/8% to reflect 1) higher GM 
assumptions as we see a milder GM dilution from N2 ramp, and 2) higher UTRs especially 
for N2/N3 given stronger demand from AI/HPC.

Reiterate Buy; TP raise to NT$3,300 from NT$3,100

earnings revisions. Our TP is based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, benchmarked to 
1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our higher 2027E EPS. Accordingly, we 
raise our 12 month TP for the ADR to US$660 from US$620, based on an unchanged FX

### Page 7

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder

promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.

Stakeholders are encouraged to consult the company's official communications or contact the
company directly for detailed information on data security policies.

中式連鎖餐飲品牌 ：八方雲集主要提供中式餐點服務 ， 擁有八方雲集 、 梁社漢排骨 ，
芳珍蔬食，丹堤咖啡、百芳池上便當、八方麵屋 6 個品牌，門店 2012 年達 500

2026 年 8 月底全球 1412，加盟佔 90%，其中台灣 1325 家，包含八方雲集 1058
家，梁社漢排骨260 家，芳珍1 家，丹堤6 家；香港71 家，包含八方雲集54 家、
百芳池上 12 家、八方麵屋 4 家、梁社漢排骨 1 家；美國 16 家八方雲集。1H26
年台灣營收占比為 77％、香港為 13%，美國佔 10%。品牌營收別八方雲集佔
74.7%，梁社漢佔 24.8%，芳珍佔 0.1%，丹堤 0.4%。1H26 台灣獲利貢獻 107%，
美國為虧損，競爭同業為王品、瓦城、美食、揚秦。

(+21%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 7.01 元。7-8 月營收 16.27 億元，預估3Q26 營收 24.2
億元(+10%YoY)，預估稅後獲利 2.41 億元(+2.6%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 3.44 元。

3

高肉品存貨，全球採購以舒緩豬價上漲的壓力。雖然今年以來包材、油品、牛肉等
產品價格上漲，但 1H26 豬肉價格下滑，支撐台灣 OP 從 1H25 的 14.7%提升到
1H26 的 16.85%。 
美國德州中央工廠和首家門市營運：美國 2025 年有 13 家據點，德州達拉斯延遲
至 2026/9 開幕(原為 2026/4)， 以快餐方式出餐的店型 ，透過提前預制縮短出餐時
間增加來客數，提高點餐的便利性吸引非亞洲族群消費，年底前德州將再展 2 家
門市。目前除了加州有中央工廠，德州中央工廠已於4 月完工啟用，可供應30-50
家門市，長期將取代加州中央工廠的功能，以降低加州中央工廠的費用分攤，短期
之內仍將呈現虧損。 
財務預估： 2026 年預估台灣營收 73.3 億元 (+7%YoY)，美國營收 9.6 億元
(+46%YoY)，預估 2026 年營收 95.2 億元(+8%YoY)，稅後獲利預估 9.7 億元
(+13%YoY)，今年配股 0.5 元，以除權後股本7 億元計算，稅後EPS 預估為 13.82
元。2027 年預估台灣營收 76.1 億元(+4%YoY)，美國營收 12.5 億元(+30%YoY)，
預估 2027 年營收 100.8 億元(+6%YoY)，稅後獲利預估 10.3 億元(+6%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 預估為 14.7 元。

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm sâu trong tuần qua, 57.87 điểm tương ứng -3.12%, với thanh khoản 
suy giảm rõ rệt. Đồ gia dụng, Hàng xa xỉ & dệt may, Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức 
giảm rõ nét nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại tiếp tục động thái bán ròng so với xu hướng của 
tuần trước với giá trị -1,634 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VPB, CTG và VCB; đồng thời, tự 
doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -317 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng 3,421 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,992 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 1,432 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 199 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần điều chỉnh đầu tiên sau 3 tuần tăng liên tiếp khi VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.795 điểm. Áp lực chốt lời sau nhịp 
hồi tích cực trong tháng 8, cộng hưởng với rủi ro căng thẳng Trung Đông leo thang là 2 
nguyên nhân chính khiến áp lực bán tăng mạnh. Nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng 4/5 
phiên trong tuần qua cho thấy dòng vốn ngoại vẫn dè dặt ngay trước thời điểm nâng hạng 
— làm dấy lên lo ngại kịch bản "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" quanh ngày 21/9. Nhìn chung, 
tâm lý thị trường chung có sự chuyển biến thận trọng trước các sự kiện quan trọng sắp 
diễn ra: diễn biến giá dầu và địa chính trị Trung Đông, cuộc họp chính sách của Fed giữa 
tháng 9, và thời điểm Việt Nam chính thức được FTSE nâng hạng lên thị trường mới nổi 
thứ cấp vào 21/9.

CTCP Tập đoàn Gemadept (GMD)  
Duy trì tăng trưởng bền vững 
03/09/2026   
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tiếp tục 
tăng trưởng tích cực

Sản lượng hàng qua cảng GMD tiếp 
tục duy trì đà tăng trưởng bền vững

Giá dịch vụ kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng 
ổn định từ nay đến 2030

Khuyến ngh ị MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
92,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần của GMD 2Q2026 đạt 1,762 tỷ VND (+18% yoy). Tổng sản lượng qua 
hệ thống cảng GMD đ ạt 1.35 tri ệu Teu (+7% yoy). LNTT 2Q2026 của GMD ghi nh ận 
1,550 tỷ VND, tăng m ạnh 129% yoy do ghi nh ận khoản lãi tài chính 60 9 tỷ VND t ừ 
chuyển nhượng cổ phần công ty liên kết. Lũy kế 6 tháng đầu năm 2026, doanh thu và 
LNTT của GMD lần lượt hoàn thành 49%/81% doanh thu và LNTT kế hoạch 2026.

Sản lượng hàng qua h ệ thống cảng GMD k ỳ vọng đạt tăng trưởng 11.6% yoy trong 
2026 và CAGR 9%/năm giai đo ạn 2026 –2030. 2H2026, Nam Đình Vũ đư ợc k ỳ vọng 
phục hồi nhờ có thêm 2 tuyến dịch vụ mới, trong khi Gemalink ti ếp tục tăng trưởng 
nhờ bổ sung tuyến mới và ngu ồn hàng ổn định từ hãng tàu đ ối tác. Triển vọng dài 
hạn được củng cố bởi dư đ ịa công su ất tại Nam Đình Vũ 3 và Gemalink 2  trong bối 
cảnh nguồn cung Cái Mép đến 2030 dự kiến vẫn thấp hơn nhu cầu.

Giá dịch vụ tại các cảng GMD kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng trung bình 5 –7%/năm tại phía 
Bắc và 7-10%/năm tại phía Nam giai đoạn 2026–2030, được hỗ trợ bởi (1) dư địa tăng 
giá còn lớn so với các nước khác trong khu vực và (2) khung trần giá dịch vụ tại cảng 
nước sâu vừa được Bộ Xây dựng nâng 10% từ 01/02/2026.

Chúng tôi khuy ến ngh ị MUA đ ối với cổ phiếu GMD, giá m ục tiêu là 92,500 VND/cổ 
phiếu, cao hơn 16.4% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 28/08/2026.

KQKD 6M2026 tăng trưởng khả quan 
so với cùng kỳ

HRC là động lực chính thúc đ ẩy tăng 
trưởng sản lượng tiêu thụ thép

Kỳ vọng giá bán thép có xu hư ớng 
được điều chỉnh tăng sau Q3/2026

Tiếp tục nâng cao kh ả năng sản xuất 
để nắm bắt các cơ hội mới

Khuyến ngh ị MUA, giá m ục tiêu 
32,150 VND/cổ phiếu 
 HPG ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST (loại trừ thu nhập từ chuyển nhượng dự án) 6M2026 
đạt lần lượt 108,060/11,538 t ỷ VND, tăng 47%/52% svck. Đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chủ 
yếu đến từ: (1) tiến độ giải ngân đầu tư công đư ợc đẩy mạnh, (2) các d ự án hạ tầng 
quy mô l ớn bước vào giai đo ạn thi công, (3) các bi ện pháp thu ế quan giúp thu h ẹp 
chênh lệch giá giữa HRC nội địa và nhập khẩu.

Chúng tôi dự báo tổng sản lượng tiêu thụ thép năm 2026/2027 đạt lần lượt 14.2/15.5 
triệu tấn. Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng bán hàng c ủa Hòa Phát đ ạt 6.8 tri ệu tấn, trong 
đó s ản lượng HRC đ ạt 3.4 tri ệu tấn tăng 57% svck, nh ờ Dung Qu ất 2 đã v ận hành 
khoảng 92% công suất thiết kế và mảng thép xây dựng tăng 17% svck nhưng các dây 
chuyền hiện tại đã hoạt động hết công suất.

Do xung đ ột chính tr ị và thắt ch ặt ngu ồn cung than c ốc, các doanh nghi ệp trong 
ngành đã điều chỉnh giá bán thép xây dựng và HRC bình quân lần lượt +8%/+12% để 
giảm áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận. KBSV kỳ vọng giá thép tăng trở lại từ Q4/2026 khi các 
doanh nghiệp trong ngành tăng cường nhập hàng để phục vụ nhu cầu xây dựng và 
tiến độ thi công đẩy nhanh vào giai đoạn cuối năm.

Hòa Phát đang triển khai đầu tư các d ự án lớn, trong đó bao g ồm (1) các h ạng mục 
thuộc Khu liên hợp Dung Quất, (2) dự án nhà máy thép ray và thép đặc biệt công suất 
700,000 tấn/năm, và (3) d ự án container. Chúng tôi gi ữ quan điểm tích c ực về triển 
vọng mở rộng danh m ục sản phẩm giá tr ị gia tăng của HPG, song v ẫn cần theo dõi 
thêm tiến độ vận hành, sản lượng thực tế và năng lực cạnh tranh được phân tích dưới 
báo cáo.

KBSV duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 32,150 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 48.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Tăng trưởng chậm lại trước chu kỳ đầu tư mới 
07/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng chậm l ại trong 
2Q2026

Lượt khách qua cảng hàng không 
Việt Nam duy trì tăng trưởng bền 
vững giai đoạn từ nay đến 2030

Số lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa của 
ACV dự kiến đạt tăng trưởng trung 
bình 6.8/3.3%/năm giai đoạn 2026-
2030

Long Thành 1 đang được đẩy nhanh 
tiến độ để kịp khai thác từ đầu 2027

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, 
giá mục tiêu 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,324 tỷ VND (-0.3% yoy), chủ yếu do (1) ACV đã 
hoàn thành chuy ển nhượng Cảng hàng không (CHK) Phú Qu ốc cho Sun Group vào 
1/1/2026 và (2) ảnh hưởng từ việc cắt giảm đường bay Trung Đông. Lũy k ế 6 tháng 
đầu năm 2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 13,164/5,278 tỷ VND 
(+4%/12% yoy).

Chúng tôi d ự phóng lư ợt khách hàng không Vi ệt Nam tăng trung bình kho ảng 
9%/năm giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nền kinh tế duy trì tăng trưởng cao, các hãng hàng 
không mở rộng đội bay, mạng bay và công suất sân bay được bổ sung liên tục, với đà 
tăng được kỳ vọng cải thiện từ 2H2026 khi áp lực từ xung đột Trung Đông và giá nhiên 
liệu giảm bớt.

Tăng trưởng hành khách quốc tế và nội địa thông qua hệ thống cảng hàng không của 
ACV dự kiến đạt CAGR lần lượt 6.8% và 3.3% giai đoạn 2026–2030, thấp hơn mức tăng 
trưởng chung của toàn thị trường hàng không Việt Nam do một phần tăng trưởng lưu 
lượng hành khách sẽ được hấp thụ bởi các sân bay ngoài hệ thống ACV trong những 
năm tới, bao gồm dự án Cảng hàng không Gia Bình và Phú Quốc.

Dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành 1 đang đư ợc đẩy nhanh tiến độ dù một số hạng mục 
vẫn còn khối lượng lớn, dự kiến hoàn thành và bắt đầu khai thác thương mại từ đầu 
2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với cổ phiếu ACV, giá mục tiêu là 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 13.7% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng được dự báo 
tiếp tục duy trì tích cực

NIM cả năm 2026, được kỳ vọng vẫn 
cải thiện nhẹ so với 2025

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ 
nhưng vẫn trong tầm kiểm soát

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
26,300 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 đạt 15%, với động lực thúc đẩy 
chính đến từ dư nợ phân khúc KHDN, đặc biệt là nhóm KHDN lớn và FDI, mặc dù nhu 
cầu đi vay của phân khúc bán l ẻ trong năm dự kiến sẽ chậm hơn do ảnh hưởng của 
mặt bằng lãi suất cao.

NIM có thể bị ảnh hưởng phần nào do tình hình huy động thị trường 1 chưa khả quan 
buộc ngân hàng phải phụ thuộc vào kênh giấy tờ có giá với chi phí cao hơn trong nửa 
cuối năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi kỳ vọng NIM của ngân hàng vẫn có thể cải thiện nhẹ so 
với cuối năm 2025 nhờ (1) ngân hàng đẩy mạnh cho vay trung dài hạn với lợi suất tốt 
hơn; (2) danh mục cho vay tiếp tục được tái định giá ở mức cao hơn trong các quý tới; 
và (3) ngân hàng có thêm nguồn huy động offshore với chi phí vốn hợp lý.

Chất lượng tài sản trong 2026 dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất 
cho vay tăng cao. Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tỷ lệ NPL sẽ tăng 13bps so với 2025 và 
tăng dự phóng đối với chi phí trích lập dự phòng so với báo cáo kỳ trước do tác 
động của Nghị định 86 lên hoạt động trích lập của ngân hàng trong nửa đầu năm 
mạnh hơn nhận định trước đó.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu ACB với giá mục tiêu 26,300 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 18% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 08/09/2026.

Chất lượng tài sản suy giảm nhẹ trong 
2H2026

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
38,750 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng của ngân hàng trong giai đoạn 
2026F-2028F lên khoảng 18-19% nhờ (1) hưởng lợi từ chính sách nới tín dụng đối 
với các dự án hạ tầng trọng điểm, (2) mối quan hệ chiến lược với các nhà thầu lớn, 
và (3) chủ động đa dạng nguồn huy động để đáp ứng nhu cầu tín dụng.

Với luận điểm ngân hàng s ẽ đẩy mạnh cho vay các d ự án hạ tầng thuộc chủ trương 
của Chính phủ và mặt bằng lãi suất đang neo ở mức cao, chúng tôi cho r ằng NIM sẽ 
tiếp tục chịu áp lực trong năm 2026, trước khi cải thiện dần trong các năm tiếp theo 
khi áp lực lãi suất hạ nhiệt và thu nhập lãi từ các khoản vay thuộc chính sách Flexible 
Pricing sẽ được thu hồi.

Chất lượng tài sản dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất neo cao gây 
áp lực lên danh mục bán lẻ của ngân hàng. Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng đối với tỷ lệ 
NPL lên thêm 10bps so với báo cáo kỳ trước và nâng nhẹ chi phí dự phòng với kỳ 
vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì bộ đệm an toàn ở mức cao.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi ti ếp tục duy trì khuy ến ngh ị MUA đối với cổ phiếu TCB v ới giá m ục tiêu 
38,750 VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 19.7% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 09/09/2026.

Tổng CTCP Bưu chính Viettel (VTP) 
Biên lợi nhuận thu hẹp trong chu kỳ mở rộng 
10/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trư ởng tích 
cực trong khi l ợi nhuận tiếp tục chịu 
áp lực

Tăng trư ởng chuy ển phát ti ếp t ục 
được h ỗ trợ bởi TMĐT và dư đ ịa gia 
tăng thị phần

Mở rộng hạ tầng và mạng lưới khu vực 
tạo đ ộng l ực tăng trư ởng m ới cho 
logistics

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP , giá mục tiêu 
56,700 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần 2Q2026 đạt 5,472 tỷ VND (+10% yoy), trong khi LNST đạt 73 tỷ 
VND, giảm 25% yoy do biên lợi nhuận thu hẹp đến từ (1) tăng chi phí nhiên liệu và 
(2) tăng chi phí vận hành, khấu hao cho các dự án đầu tư mới. Lũy kế 6T2026, 
doanh thu và LNST của VTP lần lượt đạt 10,230/112 tỷ VND, hoàn thành 52%/28% 
doanh thu và LNST kế hoạch của 2026.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng chuyển phát Việt Nam tăng 22% yoy trong 2026 và duy 
trì CAGR 15% giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nhu cầu giao nhận tiếp tục mở rộng theo đà 
tăng trưởng của thị trường TMĐT . VTP kỳ vọng tiếp tục gia tăng th ị phần lên 23.5% 
năm 2026 và đ ạt 25.5% vào 2030, duy trì t ốc độ tăng trưởng sản lượng cao hơn th ị 
trường.

VTP đang đ ẩy nhanh đ ầu tư các trung tâm logistics t ại Lạng Sơn, Hưng Yên và Đà 
Nẵng, đồng thời mở rộng hoạt động tại Lào, Campuchia và Trung Qu ốc nhằm từng 
bước hoàn thiện mạng lưới logistics xuyên biên gi ới, tạo nền tảng tham gia sâu hơn 
vào chuỗi logistics toàn trình. Chúng tôi d ự phóng doanh thu kho vận và vận tải đạt 
CAGR 22%, trong khi logistics xuyên biên gi ới tăng trung bình 29%/năm giai đo ạn 
2026–2030.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi đưa ra khuy ến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP đối với cổ phiếu VTP , với giá m ục tiêu 56,700 VND/c ổ phiếu, tương ứng 
mức tăng giá ti ềm năng 12.5%so v ới giá đóng c ửa 50,400 VND/c ổ phiếu ngày 
09/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

Nhịp tăng điểm sau kỳ nghỉ lễ đã sớm cho tín hiệu đảo chiều trong tuần qua với sự hình 
thành của một mẫu nến phân phối tiêu cực trên khung tuần. Trên khung ngày, 2 phiên 
giảm mạnh vào đầu và cuối tuần cũng cho thấy áp lực bán mang tính áp đảo với độ rộng 
thị trường hoàn toàn nghiêng về số mã giảm điểm. Với đà phân phối hiện tại, áp lực điều 
chỉnh có thể còn tiếp diễn trong những phiên kế tiếp, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn 
hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới. Ở 
góc nhìn rộng hơn, xu hướng trung hạn có phần trung tính khi chỉ số đang hình thành các 
mẫu hình vận động đi ngang theo tam giác hoặc hình bình hành.

Kháng cự gần: 1835 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1790 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1755 (+-10)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 220: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

Document 222: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 256: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-15).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

Document 313: MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 ～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCIは改革の進捗を注視～

Source PDF: all report pdf/MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 ～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCIは改革の進捗を注視～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 16 日

1/3 
MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 
～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCI は改革の進捗を注視～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ トルコ株式市場では、 投資信託による株式売却圧力を背景に下落基調が続いている。MSCI は
６月、一部投資信託による低流動性銘柄への保有集中や浮動株比率の歪みに懸念を示し、当
局に情報開示や市場監視の強化を求めた。背景には、政府が国内資金をリラ建て資産に誘導
したことで、低浮動株企業の上場や投資信託への資金流入が拡大し、低流動性銘柄の株価上
昇と資金流入が相互に増幅する構造が形成されたことがある。 
○ その後、資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）が投資信託の保有上限を厳格化し、年末までに超過分の
解消を求めたことで資金循環が逆回転した。株価下落に伴う解約増加から、一部運用会社が
解約請求に応じられない事態となり、現金確保を目的に流動性の高い銘柄にも売りが波及し
た。 当局は資金供給の拡充や投資信託の取引停止・清算のほか、 関係者を刑事告発するなど、
早期の事態収拾に向けた取り組みを強化している。 
○ MSCI は10 月15 日、 トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、2027 年６月までに十分な市場改革が進
まなければ、新興国市場からフロンティア市場などへの格下げを検討する可能性を示した。
格下げとなればパッシブ投資家を中心に資金流出が加速する懸念がある。加えて、利下げ観
測に伴うリラ安から家計資金が外貨や金に流れる可能性もあり、市場混乱の長期化が実体経
済や政府の政策運営に波及するリスクに注意が必要である。

トルコ株の下落基調が続いている（図１）（注１）。背景には、現金確保を目的に投資信託が株式
の売却圧力を強めていることがある。今年６月、指数算出会社である MSCI が一部の投資信託によ
る株式保有の集中や、流動性の低い銘柄に関する警告を発したことがきっかけとなった。 
具体的には、特定の中小上場企業と密接な関係にある投資信託の保有分が、浮動株比率の推計値
を人為的に押し上げていると、海外の機関投資家から懸念が寄せられていることを明らかにした。
こうした懸念は、当該銘柄の指数構成比率を歪めることにつながる。

注１ 10 月９日付レポート「トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666443.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
このため、同社は当局に対して、実質
的な所有者に関する詳細かつ適時の情
報開示のほか、 協調的な取引行動に対す
る強固な監視と取り締まり、 構造的に歪
められた浮動株を特定するための透明
性が高いルールに基づく枠組みの構築
を求める考えを示した。そのうえで、11
月に実施する指数見直しまでに十分か
つ具体的な進展が見られない場合、 同国
と対象銘柄の適切な取り扱いについて
協議を開始する可能性があるとした。 
こうした懸念が高まった背景には、2023 年に財務相に返り咲いたシムシェキ氏の下、 同国政府が
国内貯蓄をリラ建て資産にとどめる取り組みを進めたことがある。そのため、2021 年に導入された
為替保護型預金（ＫＫＭ：リラ安による為替下落率が預金金利を上回った場合、その差額を政府・
中銀が補填する制度）を廃止し、通常のリラ建て預金への移行を促進したほか、株式や投資信託な
どにも資金を誘導した。 
一方で、同国では高金利状態が長期化しており、政府は企業に借入ではなく、株式市場の活用に
よる資金調達の積極化を推進したため、浮動株比率が低い企業が数多く上場した。こうした銘柄は
市場で売買可能な株式数が限られ、株価が大きく変動しやすいことで、価格形成に歪みが生じた。
さらに、 その後の投資ブームを追い風に投資信託が急拡大し、 投資家にとっては株式投資の間口が
広がる一方、リスクの集中が懸念された。具体的には、投資信託に組み入れられる比較的流動性が
低い銘柄への資金流入が活発化し、 株価上昇に伴い運用収益が向上した結果、 新たな資金流入を生
む循環が形成された。 
規制機関である資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）は８月、投資信託の純資産に対する組入比率、発行済
株式数に対する保有割合など保有ポジションの上限を厳格化し、2026 年末までに超過分の解消を
義務付ける規制変更を発表した。具体的には、10 月末までに超過分の３分の１、11 月末までに３
分の２、12 月末までに完全解消を求めている。 
その結果、 株価を押し上げてきた資金流入の循環が逆回転し、 株価下落により投資信託の解約請
求が膨らんだ。 さらに、 複数の資産運用会社が投資家による解約請求に応じられないと発表する事
態に発展した。 流動性の低い保有資産の売却が困難になり、 現金確保を目的に流動性の高い銘柄に
売却圧力が強まったことで、市場全体に売り圧力が広がっている。 
市場の混乱を受けて、 中銀は流動性確保を目的とするレポ資金の供給額引き上げのほか、 銀行間
資金市場における銀行の借入限度額も大幅に引き上げた。ＳＰＫも、複数の投資信託の取引停止・
清算を命じたほか、関係者を刑事告発するといった動きをみせている。 
こうしたなか、MSCI は 10 月 15 日、トルコ株式指数に関する変更を凍結することを明らかにし
た。当局による市場改革を評価するためにさらなる時間が必要とし、2027 年６月までに十分な進展

が見られない場合は追加措置を検討するとした。

性について市場協議を行う可能性に言及した。仮に格下げが行われれば、パッシブ投資家を中心に

ある。市場の混乱が一段と長期化すれば、
実体経済への悪影響が顕在化するほか、
政府の政策運営にも影響を及ぼす可能性
があり、その行方に注意を払う必要性が
高まっている。

Document 333: Rates Spark_ Don’t expect a break back below 5%

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ Don’t expect a break back below 5%.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 3

### Page 1

We think the US 10yr stays above 5%, and in fact heads back
up to 5.25%. That won't help the eurozone, which continues to
grapple with the problem that is France. Probably overdone, but
far from over
Padhraic Garvey, CFA
We think the 10-year yield on US Treasuries will remain above 5.00% and head
back up to 5.25%
The mood for US Treasuries has calmed.
But don't get too comfortable
Treasury market moves of late have been quite something. There
were buyers in the 5.3% area post the CPI release on Wednesday.

Then the Asian open coincided with some large falls in equities,
with a particular focus on Korean equities, which prompted some
follow-on buying of Treasuries. Note, however, that there have been
decent buyers of Treasuries on an ongoing basis in the past
number of weeks. It just has not translated into a meaningful move
lower in yields, mostly as it had been oﬀset by net shorts on
Treasury futures. We've been overdue a reversal.

### Page 2

That said, we're not convinced this is a structural move, and we'd
be quite surprised if we were to break below 5% on the 10yr yield.
Treasuries did not react much to Thursday's data, but market
participants will be aware that we had another good (low) jobless
claims number, retail sales were ﬁrm and PPI inﬂation
uncomfortably high. The market will likely use this as a platform
from which to rebuild higher in yields over the weeks ahead, and
into the mid-terms; especially with neither side voicing an intention

to take action on the ﬁscal deﬁcit. The issues of high printed
inﬂation and a high deﬁcit remain.
The balance of probabilities suggests there is at least one more leg
of long-end weakness still to play out. This could take the US 10-
year yield back into the 5.25% to 5.5% range. Other markets would
likely move in tandem, mapping out similar, albeit less dramatic,
blow-outs. With 5.25% looking like a mean-reversion level, we think
the US 10-year yield could overshoot towards 5.5% and remain

elevated for a number of months as bond market jitters persist.
This could quite easily extend into the ﬁrst quarter of 2027. Our
views here are unchanged, despite recent moves.
The eurozone remains in the throes of an
uneasy holding pattern at dodgy levels
The focus in the eurozone is increasingly insular, with France front
and centre and setting the mood. Here too, there has been some
market stability – but at uncomfortable levels. The standout move

has been a doubling of the French 10yr swap spread from the 60bp
area to the 120bp area. And French spreads to Germany are
practically as wide as they've ever been since the dawn of the euro,
even versus the immediate pre-EMU years. The safe narrative to
reach for is the debt dynamics one, and the lack of ambition to
correct it. Made worse, in fact, by the political options being oﬀered
for 2028.
This is probably overdone, as a swing to the right (should it occur)

could have some tail positives that aren't currently properly
discounted. Look at the improvement seen in Italian spreads
following the rise of Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni. It's been gradual

Source PDF: all report pdf/SK海力士HBM4后道设备订单推迟至明年，扩产节奏低于预期.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3

### Page 1

SK 海⼒⼠第六代 HBM4 部分后道设备订单推迟⾄明年，⻰仁集群产能亦优先向通⽤ DRAM
倾斜，引发扩产节奏与量产忧虑。但券商与业内⼈⼠认为，后道技术问题⻛险可控，且其
已交付 16 层样品，封装优势稳固，过度恐慌并⽆充分依据。
摘要：
SK 海⼒⼠第六代⾼带宽内存 HBM4 的产能扩张步伐正在放缓，部分后道⼯序设备订单被推迟⾄
明年，市场对其量产进程的预期出现分歧。
据韩国媒体 Dealsite 援引半导体设备⾏业多位知情⼈⼠透露，原定今年下半年交付的部分 HBM4
后道设备订单存在延期迹象，后续发单计划亦尚未明确。与此同时，已交付设备的满负荷运转
情况暂⽆数据佐证，明年投资计划也尚未向部分供应商正式传达。上述动态引发市场对 SK 海⼒
⼠ HBM4 扩产节奏的担忧，相关设备供应商股价⾯临潜在压⼒。
值得注意的是，也有观点认为不宜过度解读。⼤信证券分析师柳亨根（류형근）在近期报告中指
出，市场对 SK 海⼒⼠向主要客⼾供应 HBM4 的质量问题存在 " 过度忧虑 " ，后道⼯序出现的部分问

题属于可解决范畴，尚不涉及赔偿责任。此外， SK 海⼒⼠已向主要客⼾提供 HBM4 16 层堆叠样
品，在⾼堆叠封装技术上仍处于领先地位。
设备订单推迟，扩产信号尚不明朗
据报道， SK 海⼒⼠ HBM4 部分后道⼯序设备的投资时间表近期出现明显不确定性。原计划今年
下半年落地的部分设备订单正被推迟⾄明年，后续采购计划也未⻅具体化。
⼀位⾏业⼈⼠表⽰， " 原定今年投⼊使⽤的部分设备有推迟⾄明年的迹象，已交付设备是否满负
荷运转，⽬前也尚⽆数据可供确认。 " 另⼀位⼈⼠则指出， "SK 海⼒⼠明年的投资计划似乎在内
部尚未最终敲定。 "
⽬前，今年 HBM4 相关设备的主体发单⼯作已基本收尾，但追加投资的动向仍不明显。有业界⼈
⼠指出， " 如果投资在加速推进，相关信号应该已经显现。⽬前来看， SK 海⼒⼠既没有明确缩
减、也没有明确扩⼤ HBM4 相关投资的清晰信号。 "
质量忧虑存在，但业界认为⻛险可控
部分市场观点认为，设备投资时间表的调整或与 HBM4 后道⼯序的技术问题存在关联。然⽽，⽬

前尚⽆直接证据确认两者之间的因果关系。
⼤信证券在近期报告中正⾯回应了这⼀质疑。分析师柳亨根表⽰，尽管部分市场声⾳担忧 HBM4
向主要客⼾供货的质量问题可能导致业绩低于预期，但他认为 " 这属于过度忧虑 " 。他进⼀步指
出，后道⼯序层⾯或许出现了⼀些问题，但 " 不应被视为⽆法解决的⻛险，⽬前也远未到讨论赔
偿责任的阶段 " 。
资源向通⽤ DRAM 倾斜，战略重⼼或现调整
另⼀个值得关注的背景是，近期通⽤内存价格回暖， SK 海⼒⼠的投资重⼼可能正在出现结构性
微调。

Document 368: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics
